_source line 4468 · 2026-05-23T15:01:47.580468+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] 补充引用 — R17 建议的新引用

## 1. 确认 Haghighat 已引用

检查 references.bib 是否已有 Haghighat et al. "Resolving Predictive Multiplicity for the Rashomon Set" (AAAI 2026)。R16 已将此条目更新为 AAAI 2026 格式。如果已存在，无需重复添加。

## 2. 新增 Ye et al. (2026)

添加到 references.bib：
```bibtex
@article{ye2026provable,
  title={A Provable Expressiveness Hierarchy in Hybrid Linear-Full Attention},
  author={Ye, Xiaowei and He, Xiaoyu and Liao, Chao and Wu, Chen and Lu, Pinyan},
  journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2602.01763},
  year={2026}
}
```

在 Related Work §2 的 hybrid architecture 段落中自然引入，如："Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level."

## 3. 跳过 Heilmann et al.

Federated learning 场景距离过远，不添加。

编译后确认无 warnings。

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} and Hyena \citep{poli2023hyena} establish early design points in the hybrid space with gated convolutions and subquadratic operators. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear attention \citep{katharopoulos2020transformers} through the state-space--attention duality of Mamba-2.
8	
9	\paragraph{Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity.}
10	\citet{damour2022underspecification} formalized underspecification: eq

[Director 指令] [Patch] 最终提交前的 5 处微调，Reviewer 终审识别。全部是文字替换，不涉及实验数据。这次不用验证引用。

逐条修改清单：

1. **Introduction 首句拆分**（introduction.tex）
   当前首句约 42 词过长，拆成两句：
   - "Hybrid linear recurrent architectures—including GDN, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, and DeltaNet—achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1–2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets."
   - 第二句接："Yet they can differ on up to 90\% of the individual examples they affect."

2. **Abstract "mask" 措辞**（main.tex abstract）
   找到 "can mask per-example consequences"，替换为 "can mask which specific examples each architecture gets right"

3. **Related Work 末句重写**（related_work.tex）
   找到 "The contribution lies not in..." 开头的句子，替换为："We provide the first controlled quantification of this phenomenon across recurrent architectural families, establishing its magnitude and task-dependence under matched training conditions."

4. **四处 convergence 残留替换**：

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid recurrent architectures that appear equivalent by aggregate benchmarks affect largely non-overlapping sets of individual examples: adding attention to three controlled linear recurrent backbones yields helped and hurt sets with at most 10\% pairwise overlap, approximately twice the independence baseline. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics: aggregate cross-architecture variance decreases on some benchmarks as attention ratio increases, but each architecture reaches similar accuracy by gaining and losing accuracy on different inputs. Practitioners increasingly choose between recurrent backbones based on aggregate benchmark scores. Across all ten pairwise comparisons among five architectures, overlap in per-example effects remains below 14\% (all $p < .01$), meaning the choice of backbone is associated with which specific inputs a deployed system handles correctly.
4	
5	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. These systems now serve as production-grade alternatives to pure Transformers, and the practical question of how much attention to include is typically answered by comparing aggregate benchmark numbers. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide a controlled model zoo spanning three recurrent architectures, five attention ratios, and two parameter scales, which enables systematic study of how attention allocation affects these hybrid designs.

_source line 4494 · 2026-05-23T16:48:28.952747+00:00_

24	
25	\begin{abstract}
26	Practitioners select among hybrid linear recurrent architectures using aggregate benchmarks, but these scores can mask per-example consequences. Across three controlled designs, adding attention affects substantially different examples per architecture (Jaccard $\approx$ 0.10, approximately twice the independence baseline); validation across five architectures and ten pairwise comparisons confirms this on all four benchmarks (all $p < .01$). Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that this predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) more than attention dose. Aggregate trends do not reach statistical significance ($n = 5$; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$), yet per-example overlap saturates at 4\% attention and remains flat across all four benchmarks. Majority vote fails to exploit this complementarity, confirming that configurations interchangeable by aggregate metrics carry hidden per-example consequences. Confidence-informed routing extracts 36\% of oracle complementarity on LAMBADA ($+5.3$\,pp vs.\ majority vote), suggesting these differences are partially actionable.
27	\end{abstract}
28

9	\paragraph{Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity.}
10	\citet{damour2022underspecification} formalized underspecification: equivalent aggregate performance can mask per-prediction divergence. Concrete NLP instances include seed-dependent BERT disagreement \citep{mccoy2020berts,sellam2022multiberts,dodge2020finetuning} and the four-phase convergence pattern of autoregressive LMs \citep{fehlauer2025convergence}. \citet{marx2020predictive} introduced predictive multiplicity as a complementary lens (extending prediction churn; \citealt{milanifard2016launch}), showing that near-optimal models produce conflicting per-example predictions; extensions address probabilistic classification \citep{watson2023predictive}, Rashomon capacity \citep{hsu2022rashomon}, reconciliation \citep{du2025reconciling,haghighat2026resolving}, and cross-model disagreement as a correctness signal \citep{gorbett2026crossmodel}. \citet{swayamdipta2020dataset} characterize examples via training dynamics (variability across epochs); our decomposition operates at deployment time across architectures with no training signal. \citet{niven2019probing} show that benchmark artifacts can inflate per-example agreement; our cross-benchmark variation ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) suggests the patterns we observe are not driven by a single artifact type. \citet{fort2019deep} provide a loss landscape perspective on deep ensembles, showing that diverse architectures explore distinct modes and produce disagreement on individual predictions; standard ensemble diversity measures \citep{kuncheva2003measures}, including the Q-statistic \citep{brown2005diversity}, quantify such disagreement; Jaccard similarity is one instance. \citet{black2022model} survey the model multiplicity landscape, \citet{ganesh2025systemizing} systemize sources of arbitrariness, and \citet{cooper2024arbitrariness} connect predictive arbitrariness to fairness. These studies attribute divergence to initialization noise or the near-optimal

_source line 4502 · 2026-05-23T16:48:31.970100+00:00_

108	\subsection{Robustness}\label{ssec:robustness}
109	
110	Removing GDN weakens the LAMBADA variance reduction pattern ($\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$) and reverses MMLU's ($\rho = +0.6$), both driven by GDN's 16-head configuration (vs.\ 32 in others; Appendix~\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The RetNet--HGRN2 pair preserves decoupling ($\sigma \leq 0.008$, $J = 0.086$--$0.108$). The aggregate variance pattern is therefore dependent on GDN inclusion; the decoupling result is not. Five-architecture validation at 340M (GLA, DeltaNet added) confirms $J \leq 0.14$ across all ten pairs and four benchmarks (all $p < .01$; Figure~\ref{fig:jaccard_heatmap_5arch}; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}). Three-architecture results are stable across both 340M and 1.3B scales (Jaccard $\approx$ 0.08--0.10), providing indirect support for scale robustness. Full sensitivity analyses appear in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}.
111

[Director 指令] [Patch] Appendix P.2 正态性验证段落更新（基于 Shapiro-Wilk 实测数据）。这次不用验证引用。

找到 Appendix P.2 中现有的正态性验证段落（之前写的版本），替换为以下更精确的版本：

"Shapiro-Wilk tests reject normality for all six architecture–set combinations (all $W < 0.98$, $p < 10^{-6}$), but the practical departure varies by group. Disruption-set $|\log p|$ values show mild skewness (0.53--0.77, $W \in [0.955, 0.973]$), closely approximating a normal distribution at the sample sizes involved. Rescue-set values are moderately right-skewed (skewness 1.33--1.41, $W \in [0.899, 0.920]$), following an approximately log-normal distribution. Because the skew is one-sided, parametric AUC estimates (0.70--0.74 in the main text) are conservative lower bounds; non-parametric empirical AUC from the same data yields 0.73--0.77."

编译确认无错误。

_source line 4524 · 2026-05-23T16:50:31.285239+00:00_

539	\end{table}
540	
541	\paragraph{Normality verification.} We verify the normality assumption underlying the parametric AUC estimate by computing empirical (non-parametric) AUC directly from the data. For disruption prediction, empirical AUC values (0.73--0.75) differ from parametric estimates by less than 2\%, confirming that the normality assumption is adequate. For rescue prediction, empirical AUC values (0.76) exceed parametric estimates (0.70--0.71) by approximately 7\%, indicating mild right-skew in the $|\log p|$ distribution. The parametric estimate is therefore conservative (understates predictive power), and the main-text AUC range of 0.70--0.74 represents a lower bound.
542	
543	Despite similar within-architecture AUC, the optimal prediction thresholds differ across architectures for disruption but not rescue (Table~\ref{tab:threshold_gap}). Rescue thresholds cluster tightly (range 0.03 in $|\log p|$), indicating that the rescuability boundary---the confidence level below which pure-wrong examples are likely to be corrected by attention---is architecture-invariant. Disruption thresholds span a 47\% relative range (GDN: 0.70, RetNet: 1.00, HGRN2: 1.14), meaning that the same confidence level on a pure-correct example may predict vulnerability in one architecture but safety in another. This asymmetry constitutes a second dimension of multiplicity beyond per-example disagreement: all three architectures agree on which wrong answers are rescuable, but disagree on which correct answers are vulnerable to disruption.
544

775	All pairwise Jaccard values remain $\leq 0.14$, consistent with the three-architecture results at 1.3B (Table~\ref{tab:per_example}). The seven new pairs involving GLA and DeltaNet show no systematic difference from the original three pairs (Mann-Whitney $p > .3$ for both helped and hurt), indicating that the low-overlap pattern is not specific to the original architecture triple. A sixth architecture (HGRN; \citealp{qin2023hierarchically}) is also available in the zoo but excluded from the main analysis: its substantially lower baseline accuracy on LAMBADA (0.263 vs.\ 0.32--0.38 for the other five) mechanically inflates helped-set sizes and Jaccard values. All 15 six-architecture permutation tests nonetheless remain significant ($z \geq 2.9$, $p \leq .006$). The same pattern holds on HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$): all 20 additional pairwise tests yield $p < .01$ with mean $J_{\text{helped}} \leq 0.10$. Full four-benchmark and six-architecture data are included in our code release.

39	\paragraph{Confidence margin predictor.} Pure-model confidence on the target token predicts which examples will be affected (AUC 0.70--0.74; Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}): rescue thresholds are architecture-invariant (1\% spread) but disruption thresholds vary 47\%, explaining cross-architecture Jaccard divergence (Appendix~\ref{app:prediction_framework}). Calibration differences account for less than half the threshold spread (Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}). The rescue pattern generalizes across three benchmarks ($d = 0.13$--$0.60$; Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}); disruption is weaker on reasoning tasks (Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}). This structured disagreement constitutes predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} under architectural variation \citep[cf.][]{damour2022underspecification}.
40	
41	\paragraph{Confidence-based routing.} As an illustrative case study, the rescue-threshold asymmetry suggests a practical routing strategy: for each example, select the architecture whose pure-model confidence best predicts successful rescue. On LAMBADA, rescue-threshold routing achieves 56.2\% accuracy versus 50.9\% for majority vote ($+5.3$\,pp, SD $= 0.8$\,pp across 10 splits), extracting 36\% of the oracle gap (oracle = 65.5\%). On ARC-Easy, gains are modest ($+1.0$\,pp, SD $= 0.9$\,pp); on HellaSwag and PIQA, routing provides no benefit. We note that confidence-weighted majority vote and other ensemble strategies may provide comparable or superior gains; systematic comparison is left to future work. Using 10-repeat 50/50 cross-validated splits, routing works when multiplicity is high ($\alpha = 0.30$, LAMBADA) and a clear rescue-threshold signal exists, but fails when multiplicity is low ($\alpha = 0.14$, PIQA).
42

_source line 4547 · 2026-05-23T17:09:58.335992+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Per-example multiplicity in hybrid recurrent models varies by task ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$), with Jaccard uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$) even as aggregate variance decreases on LAMBADA (non-significant; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}) (dependent on GDN inclusion; \S\ref{ssec:robustness}); five-architecture validation confirms this across four benchmarks (all $J \leq 0.14$, $p < .01$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}). Pure-model confidence partially predicts affected examples (AUC 0.70--0.74; \S\ref{ssec:per_example}).
4	
5	At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, two implications for practitioners follow from the ${\sim}90\%$ non-overlap: (1) $\alpha > 0.2$ warrants per-example audit before architecture substitution, as multiplicity at this level means $>$20\% of examples change outcome; (2) low-Jaccard pairs maximize ensemble complementarity---majority vote fails despite 15--30\% disagreement, but oracle accuracy reveals $+7$--$14$\,pp of untapped gain. Preliminary results suggest rescue-threshold routing may partially exploit this complementarity on high-$\alpha$ tasks ($+5.3$\,pp on LAMBADA; \S\ref{ssec:per_example}), but further validation across tasks and scales is needed.
6

64	\subsection{Aggregate Trends and Per-Example Independence}\label{ssec:decoupling}
65	
66	The per-example findings above raise a natural question: when aggregate metrics converge, does this reflect agreement on the same examples? Table~\ref{tab:convergence} provides context. Only LAMBADA shows a monotonically decreasing variance pattern ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN, whose h3-1 uses 16 rather than 32 attention heads: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Appendix~\ref{app:convergence_detail}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). This is a suggestive but underpowered secondary observation ($n = 5$; the test has negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$). Given the limited statistical power, non-significant correlations on other benchmarks should not be interpreted as absence of association. LAMBADA relies purely on in-context retrieval without external knowledge or multi-step reasoning, making it the task most directly aligned with attention's role in context access---a plausible explanation for why convergence is strongest here.
67

69	\centering
70	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_task_specificity.pdf}

Bootstrap CI is heuristic given $n = 3$ architectures.}
72	\label{fig:task_specificity}
73	\end{figure}

646	
647	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Scale Comparison}
648	
649	Table~\ref{tab:340m_multi_bench} extends the scale comparison to all four main benchmarks. Two patterns emerge. First, $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M across all benchmarks, with the largest gap on ARC-Easy ($+$8.4pp) and the smallest on LAMBADA ($+$0.6pp). Second, pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\bar{J}_h = 0.08$--$0.10$ at both scales), confirming that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---is scale-robust even as its magnitude increases at smaller scale. Notably, the $\alpha$ inflation does not track accuracy loss: HellaSwag has the largest accuracy gap between scales yet the smallest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$1.9pp), while ARC-Easy has a smaller accuracy gap but the largest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$8.4pp), arguing against accuracy as the primary driver of scale-dependent multiplicity.

_source line 4585 · 2026-05-23T17:52:13.761630+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Per-example multiplicity in hybrid recurrent models varies by task ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$), with Jaccard uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$) even as aggregate variance decreases on LAMBADA (non-significant; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}) (dependent on GDN inclusion; \S\ref{ssec:robustness}); five-architecture validation confirms this across four benchmarks (all $J \leq 0.14$, $p < .01$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}). Pure-model confidence partially predicts affected examples (AUC 0.70--0.74; \S\ref{ssec:per_example}).
4	
5	At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, two implications for practitioners follow from the ${\sim}90\%$ non-overlap: (1) $\alpha > 0.2$ warrants per-example audit before architecture substitution, as multiplicity at this level means $>$20\% of examples change outcome; (2) low-Jaccard pairs maximize ensemble complementarity---majority vote fails despite 15--30\% disagreement, but oracle accuracy reveals $+7$--$14$\,pp of untapped gain. Preliminary results suggest rescue-threshold routing may partially exploit this complementarity on high-$\alpha$ tasks ($+5.3$\,pp on LAMBADA; \S\ref{ssec:per_example}), but routing is counterproductive on low-$\alpha$ tasks ($-0.49$\,pp on PIQA), and further validation across tasks and scales is needed.
6

Rescue-threshold routing partially exploits this on high-$\\alpha$ tasks ($+5.3$\\,pp on LAMBADA) but is counterproductive on low-$\\alpha$ tasks ($-0.49$\\,pp on PIQA)."

_source line 4609 · 2026-05-23T18:32:20.323303+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Per-example multiplicity in hybrid recurrent models varies by task ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$), with Jaccard uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$). Pure-model confidence partially predicts affected examples (AUC 0.70--0.74). At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, two implications follow: (1) $\alpha > 0.2$ warrants per-example audit before architecture substitution; (2) low-Jaccard pairs maximize ensemble complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain beyond majority vote). Rescue-threshold routing partially exploits this on high-$\alpha$ tasks ($+5.3$\,pp on LAMBADA) but is counterproductive on low-$\alpha$ tasks ($-0.49$\,pp on PIQA).
4

[Director 指令] [Patch] 数据自查发现的 3 个修复。这次不用验证引用。

**1. [必改] Appendix ECE spread 百分比错误**
在 appendix.tex 中找到 "The cross-architecture ECE spread (17%, computed as range/mean)"，将 "17%" 改为 "6%"。实际值为 5.7%（range=0.013, mean=0.227），17% 是计算错误。

**2. [必改] Table 11 caption 描述不准确**
Table 11 caption 中 "Mean pairwise Jaccard (**helped sets**, 3 architecture pairs)" 改为 "Mean pairwise Jaccard (**mean of helped and hurt sets**, 3 architecture pairs)"。表中数据实际是 helped+hurt 平均值，不仅是 helped。

**3. [建议改] Routing 数值统一来源**
§4.1 routing 段当前混用两个来源。统一使用 paired t-test 值（因为已报 paired p-values）：
- LAMBADA: "+5.3\,pp" → "+5.2\,pp"
- ARC-Easy: "+1.0\,pp" → "+1.2\,pp"
- HellaSwag (-0.06pp) 和 PIQA (-0.49pp) 保持不变（已是 paired 值）
- Abstract 中的 "+5.3 pp" 也同步改为 "+5.2 pp"
- 检查 Conclusion 和其他引用这两个数字的地方，一并更新

编译确认主文仍 ≤ 8 页。

_source line 4645 · 2026-05-23T19:36:57.587593+00:00_

560	\section{Calibration Analysis}\label{app:calibration}
561	
562	Cross-architecture disruption threshold variation (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}) could in principle arise from calibration differences rather than genuine differences in per-example processing. We compute expected calibration error (ECE; \citealt{guo2017calibration}) for each architecture's h3-1 model on LAMBADA using 15 equal-width bins. ECE values are similar: GDN 0.222, RetNet 0.224, HGRN2 0.235. The cross-architecture ECE spread (17\%, computed as range/mean) is less than half the disruption threshold spread (47\%; Table~\ref{tab:threshold_gap}), indicating that calibration differences are insufficient to account for the observed threshold divergence. Rescue thresholds, which show only 1\% cross-architecture spread, are consistent with near-identical calibration in the low-confidence regime.
563	
564	\section{BoolQ Supplementary Analysis}

6. **app:complementarity** — 在 §4.1 routing 段落提到 oracle gap 时，加 "(complementarity decomposition in Appendix~\ref{app:complementarity})"

**注意页数**：这些都是括号内的简短引用，每个 <1 行，但总共 6 处可能加 2-3 行。确认主文仍 ≤ 8 页。如果页数紧张，优先补 app:per_example_counts、app:arceasy、app:scale_convergence 这三个最重要的。

## 修改清单（按优先级排序）

### P0-1: C1 叙事重构（Critical，跨 Abstract/Intro/§4.2/Conclusion）

核心问题：论文叙事预设 "aggregate convergence masks per-example divergence"，但 aggregate convergence 统计不显著（Bonferroni p=.170）。R18 两位 Reviewer 一致 Critical。

修复方向：重构为 "architectures interchangeable by aggregate metrics differ substantially at per-example level"。不依赖 convergence trend，而是强调 aggregate 分数的不可区分性。

具体改动：
1. **§4.2 标题** "Aggregate Trends and Per-Example Independence" → "Aggregate Variance and Per-Example Overlap"（或类似非确定性标题）
2. **Abstract**: 审查所有暗示 convergence 存在的措辞，替换为 interchangeability 语言。"Aggregate trends do not reach statistical significance" 可以保留但确保不暗示 convergence 本身是核心 claim
3. **Introduction**: "Yet they can differ on up to 90%" 的 framing 可保留——它不依赖 convergence。检查其他地方
4. **§4.2 正文**: 所有暗示 convergence 是 established fact 的措辞加限定词（"non-significantly"、"suggestive"）
5. **Conclusion**: "appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks" 已对，保持。检查其他确认性语言

### P0-2: C3 Bootstrap CI 改造（Critical，§3.5 + §4.2 + Figure 2 caption）

核心问题：n=3 架构的 bootstrap 95% CI（如 [-1.0, -0.7]）传递虚假精度感。R18 从 Warning 升级为 Critical。

修复：
1. §4.2 所有 bootstrap CI 处加 "heuristic" 限定

### P0-3: M6 Abstract 精简

数字密度过高。保留 3-4 个关键数值（Jaccard ≈ 0.10、α = 0.14-0.30、+5.2 pp），其余用文字描述替代。注意 200 word 上限。

### P0-4: C2 因果语言弱化（全文）

单次训练 run 不支持因果归因。搜索替换：
- "architectural variation produces" → "architectural variation is associated with"
- "adding attention affects substantially different examples" → "adding attention is associated with substantially different per-example effects"
- 其他 "produces", "causes", "drives" 等因果动词 → associative language
- Introduction contributions 和 Related Work 最后一段是重点

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures---including GDN, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, and DeltaNet---achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets. Yet they can differ on up to 90\% of the individual examples they affect: adding attention to three controlled linear recurrent backbones yields helped and hurt sets with at most 10\% pairwise overlap, approximately twice the independence baseline. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics, which can decrease as attention increases even though each architecture reaches similar accuracy by gaining and losing on different inputs. Across all ten pairwise comparisons among five architectures, overlap in per-example effects remains below 14\% (all $p < .01$), meaning the choice of backbone determines which specific inputs a deployed system handles correctly.
4	
5	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. These systems now serve as production-grade alternatives to pure Transformers, and the practical question of how much attention to include is typically answered by comparing aggregate benchmark numbers. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide a controlled model zoo spanning three recurrent architectures, five attention ratios, and two parameter scales, which enables systematic study of how attention allocation affects these hybrid designs.
6	
7	Prior work on predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} and underspecification \citep{damour2022underspecification,mccoy2020berts} documents this risk for seed variation within a single architecture, but whether it extends to architectural variation in

_source line 4719 · 2026-05-24T12:27:20.743538+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose, with pairwise Jaccard uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$) and saturated at the first attention level. Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	Two practical implications follow. First, when $\alpha > 0.2$, per-example audit is warranted before substituting one architecture for another---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Second, the low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), but exploiting it requires task-appropriate routing; confidence-based selection is effective on high-$\alpha$ tasks (LAMBADA: $+5.2$\,pp) but counterproductive on low-$\alpha$ ones (PIQA: $-0.49$\,pp). Extending these analyses to instruction-tuned models and generation tasks is a natural next step.
7

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} and Hyena \citep{poli2023hyena} establish early design points in the hybrid space with gated convolutions and subquadratic operators. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear a

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.
4	
5	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}
6	
7	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, which provides controlled comparisons across architectures and attention ratios. The zoo contains three linear recurrent architectures: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2}. These three architectures represent distinct approaches to linear recurrence: GatedDeltaNet uses gated delta rules, RetNet uses multi-scale exponential decay, and HGRN2 uses gated linear recurrence with state expansion. Each architecture is trained at five attention ratios: 0\% (pure recurrent), 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, and 25\%. We adopt the h$k$-1 notation from the original work, where one attention layer is interleaved per every $k{+}1$ total layers: h3-1 corresponds to 25\% attention, h6-1 to 14.3\%, h12-1 to 7.7\%, and h24-1 to 4\%. All configurations are available at two scales: 340M and 1.3B parameters. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters; they differ only in architecture and attention configuration. This controlled design isolates the effect of attention from confounds such as training data or optimization differences. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline (pure attention) at both scales.
8	
9	\subsection{Task Taxonomy}\label{ssec:task_taxonomy}
10	
11	We evaluate on 10 benchmarks grouped into four categories defined by the primary cognitive demand of each task. This taxonomy (informal; not pre-registered) was fixed prior to analysis following established distinctions in the hybrid architecture literature \citep{arora2024zoology} (see Appendix~\ref{app:benchmark_details} for the confirmatory/exploratory split), not der

```json
  "old_string": "Practitioners select among hybrid linear recurrent architectures using aggregate benchmarks, but these scores can mask which specific examples each architecture gets right. Across three controlled designs, adding attention affects substantially different examples per architecture (Jaccard $\\approx$ 0.10, approximately twice the independence baseline); validation across five architectures and ten pairwise comparisons confirms this on all four benchmarks (all $p < .01$). Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that this predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) more than attention dose. Aggregate trends do not reach statistical significance ($n = 5$; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$), yet per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat across all four benchmarks. Majority vote fails to exploit this complementarity, confirming that configurations interchangeable by aggregate metrics carry hidden per-example consequences. Confidence-informed routing extracts 36\\% of oracle complementarity on LAMBADA ($+5.2$\\,pp vs.\\ majority vote), suggesting these differences are partially actionable.",
  "new_string": "Practitioners select among hybrid linear recurrent architectures using aggregate benchmarks, but architectures that appear interchangeable by these metrics can differ on which specific examples they get right. Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that adding attention is associated with substantially different per-example changes across architectures (Jaccard $\\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) more than attention dose. Per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat, even as aggregate variance shows a non-significant decreasing trend on one benchmark (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$). Majority vote fails to exploit the resulting complementarity. Confidence-informed routing extracts 36\\% of oracle complementarity on LAMBADA ($+5.2$\\,pp), suggesting these differences are partially actionable on high-multiplicity tasks."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "Two practical implications follow. First, when $\\alpha > 0.2$, per-example audit is warranted before substituting one architecture for another---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Second, the low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\\,pp oracle gain), but exploiting it requires task-appropriate routing; confidence-based selection is effective on high-$\\alpha$ tasks (LAMBADA: $+5.2$\\,pp) but counterproductive on low-$\\alpha$ ones (PIQA: $-0.49$\\,pp). Extending these analyses to instruction-tuned models and generation tasks is a natural next step.",
  "new_string": "Two observations follow at the 340M--1.3B scales studied here. First, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Second, the low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\\,pp oracle gain), but our preliminary routing experiments suggest that exploiting it is task-dependent: confidence-based selection shows gains on high-multiplicity free-form tasks (LAMBADA: $+5.2$\\,pp) but is counterproductive on low-multiplicity ones (PIQA: $-0.49$\\,pp)."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "LAMBADA is the only free-form generation benchmark in our evaluation; its higher $\\alpha$ (0.30 vs.\\ 0.14--0.21 for multiple-choice tasks) may partly reflect the larger answer space inherent to open-ended prediction. However, within multiple-choice tasks, format (two-way vs.\\ four-way) does not predict $\\alpha$ (\\S\\ref{ssec:per_example}).",
  "new_string": "LAMBADA is the only free-form generation benchmark in our evaluation; all others use multiple-choice scoring. Its higher $\\alpha$ (0.30 vs.\\ 0.14--0.21 for multiple-choice tasks) may partly reflect the larger answer space inherent to open-ended prediction, and the multiple-choice format may mechanically constrain per-example divergence by limiting the number of distinct error patterns. We cannot fully disentangle task type from evaluation format with the present benchmarks. However, within multiple-choice tasks, format (two-way vs.\\ four-way) does not predict $\\alpha$ (\\S\\ref{ssec:per_example}), suggesting that format alone does not drive the observed variation."
}
```

650	
651	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Scale Comparison}
652	
653	Table~\ref{tab:340m_multi_bench} extends the scale comparison to all four main benchmarks. Two patterns emerge. First, $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M across all benchmarks, with the largest gap on ARC-Easy ($+$8.4pp) and the smallest on LAMBADA ($+$0.6pp). Second, pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\bar{J}_h = 0.08$--$0.10$ at both scales); three-architecture results are stable across both 340M and 1.3B scales (Jaccard $\approx$ 0.08--0.10), providing indirect support for scale robustness. This confirms that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---is scale-robust even as its magnitude increases at smaller scale. Notably, the $\alpha$ inflation does not track accuracy loss: HellaSwag has the largest accuracy gap between scales yet the smallest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$1.9pp), while ARC-Easy has a smaller accuracy gap but the largest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$8.4pp), arguing against accuracy as the primary driver of scale-dependent multiplicity.
654

_source line 4797 · 2026-05-24T12:32:10.946987+00:00_

653	Table~\ref{tab:340m_multi_bench} extends the scale comparison to all four main benchmarks. Two patterns emerge. First, $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M across all benchmarks, with the largest gap on ARC-Easy ($+$8.4pp) and the smallest on LAMBADA ($+$0.6pp). Second, pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\bar{J}_h = 0.08$--$0.10$ at both scales); three-architecture results are stable across both 340M and 1.3B scales (Jaccard $\approx$ 0.08--0.10), providing indirect support for scale robustness. This suggests that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---is consistent across the two scales examined, even as its magnitude increases at smaller scale. Notably, the $\alpha$ inflation does not track accuracy loss: HellaSwag has the largest accuracy gap between scales yet the smallest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$1.9pp), while ARC-Easy has a smaller accuracy gap but the largest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$8.4pp), arguing against accuracy as the primary driver of scale-dependent multiplicity.
654

Figure~\ref{fig:jaccard_hierarchy} shows a three-tier Jaccard hierarchy: independence baseline (${\sim}0.05$), cross-architecture (${\sim}0.10$, $2\times$ baseline), and within-architecture (${\sim}0.33$, $3\times$ cross-architecture; Appendix~\ref{app:within_arch_stability}). Pythia 70M seed calibration produces a comparable within-seed level ($J = 0.27$--$0.39$; Appendix~\ref{app:seed_calibration}), consistent with architecture-driven divergence, though matched multi-seed experiments would be needed for definitive evidence.

### Tool result

_source line 4811 · 2026-05-24T12:32:46.804851+00:00_

24	    \item Architectural variation---a source of multiplicity not previously characterized at the per-example level---is associated with structured predictive multiplicity ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) across four benchmarks, with magnitude determined by task type rather than attention dose (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}, Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}). Validation across five architectures confirms the pattern: all ten pairwise Jaccard values remain $\leq 0.14$ (all $p < .01$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}). The primary controlled variable is the recurrent backbone \citep[cf.][]{mccoy2020berts, damour2022underspecification}, though single training runs prevent full disentanglement from seed effects (Limitations).
25	    \item Per-example Jaccard saturates at the first attention level (4\%) across all four benchmarks, while aggregate variance shows a non-significant decreasing pattern on LAMBADA (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_bench_jaccard}). Minimal attention suffices to establish the full pattern of configuration-specific per-example effects; additional attention narrows aggregate gaps without homogenizing which examples each architecture affects. The aggregate variance pattern depends on GDN inclusion (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}) and does not replicate on other benchmarks.

[Director 指令] [Patch 补充] Reviewer 独立审查反馈，请合并到正在执行的 R18 修复中：

## C1 叙事重构的关键调整

**不要完全消除 σ 下降描述**——否则 decoupling 发现失去对照项。正确做法：将 σ 下降从 **finding** 降级为 **background context/question premise**。

具体：§4.2 开头改为类似 "On LAMBADA, cross-architecture σ decreases from 0.024 to 0.005 (Table 1; Bonferroni-adjusted p = .170, non-significant). Regardless of whether this reflects a genuine trend, we ask: does any aggregate narrowing correspond to per-example agreement?"

**Figure 1 caption** 也需配合：当前暗示 convergence 是 finding。改为 "Descriptive trajectories of aggregate variance and per-example overlap" 或类似非确定性措辞。

## C3 Bootstrap CI 的修正

不要在正文列 3 个 σ 数据点——**Table 1 已经展示了全部数据**。更清晰的做法：§4.2 或 Figure 2 caption 中写 "readers can verify ρ directly from Table 1; with n = 3, all bootstrap CIs are heuristic indicators of direction rather than precise coverage"。

## M1 Routing 补充

恢复 routing 段开头的 "As an illustrative case study" 措辞（之前删掉了），让读者第一眼知道这不是 core contribution。加 1 句 ensemble baseline limitation："A natural comparison—average-logits ensemble—is left to future work."

## M5 引用验证

**Jiang et al. (2024) "On the Predictive Multiplicity of Foundation Models" 需要验证真实性再添加。** 如果无法确认，不要加。Wortsman et al. (2022) Model Soups (ICML 2022) 是确认存在的。

## 新增任务：前四页视觉密度不足

用户反馈：前四页只有一张小图（Figure 1），冲击力不够。Pages 3-4（Related Work 尾部 + 全部 Method §3.1-3.5）是纯文本沙漠。

解决方案——在 §3 Method 中加入一个方法概览图。Main Agent 正在创建这个图（Figure 2: Experimental Framework），你预留位置。具体放置：§3.1 Model Zoo 之后或 §3.4 Per-Example Decomposition 之前，占 1/3 到 1/2 页高度。我会在图生成后通知你集成。

同时考虑：Figure 1 是否可以适当增大？如果当前较小，可以调整。

71	\subsection{Aggregate Variance and Per-Example Overlap}\label{ssec:decoupling}
72	
73	The per-example findings raise a natural question: do architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate metrics agree on the same examples? Table~\ref{tab:convergence} shows that only LAMBADA exhibits a monotonically decreasing variance pattern ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN, whose h3-1 uses 16 rather than 32 attention heads: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Appendix~\ref{app:convergence_detail}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). This is a suggestive but underpowered observation ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$). LAMBADA relies purely on in-context retrieval without external knowledge or multi-step reasoning, making it the task most directly aligned with attention's role in context access.

47	
48	\paragraph{Confidence-based routing.} The rescue-threshold asymmetry suggests a practical routing strategy: select the architecture whose pure-model confidence best predicts successful rescue. On LAMBADA, this outperforms majority vote by $+5.2$\,pp (SD $= 0.4$\,pp, 10 cross-validated splits), extracting 36\% of the oracle gap (oracle = 65.5\%; Appendix~\ref{app:complementarity}). On ARC-Easy, gains are modest ($+1.2$\,pp, SD $= 0.5$\,pp); on HellaSwag, routing provides no benefit ($-0.06$\,pp, $p = .374$); on PIQA, it is significantly negative ($-0.49$\,pp, $p = .006$). Routing works when multiplicity is high ($\alpha = 0.30$, LAMBADA) and a clear rescue-threshold signal exists, but is counterproductive when multiplicity is low ($\alpha = 0.14$, PIQA).
49	
50	\paragraph{Multiplicity variation.} Extending to HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$) reveals that predictive multiplicity varies systematically across tasks (Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}). The ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) through 0.15 (HellaSwag) and 0.21 (ARC-Easy; Appendix~\ref{app:arceasy}) to 0.30 (LAMBADA). Format does not drive this ordering: HellaSwag (four-way) and PIQA (two-choice) produce nearly identical $\alpha$ despite different formats, and $\alpha$ does not correlate with task accuracy ($\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$). Bootstrap 95\% CIs confirm the ordering (all non-overlapping except PIQA--HellaSwag; Table~\ref{tab:arceasy_ambiguity}). Pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$ across four benchmarks), indicating that architectures affect different examples regardless of how many are affected. Within each benchmark, $\alpha$ is stable across attention ratios (variation $< 0.03$) but differs $2\times$ across task types, confirming that multiplicity magnitude depends on task, not attention dose \citep[cf.][]{gorbett2026crossmodel}.

7	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, which provides controlled comparisons across architectures and attention ratios. The zoo contains three linear recurrent architectures: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2}. These three architectures represent distinct approaches to linear recurrence: GatedDeltaNet uses gated delta rules, RetNet uses multi-scale exponential decay, and HGRN2 uses gated linear recurrence with state expansion. Each architecture is trained at five attention ratios: 0\% (pure recurrent), 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, and 25\%. We adopt the h$k$-1 notation from the original work, where one attention layer is interleaved per every $k{+}1$ total layers: h3-1 corresponds to 25\% attention, h6-1 to 14.3\%, h12-1 to 7.7\%, and h24-1 to 4\%. All configurations are available at two scales: 340M and 1.3B parameters. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters; they differ only in architecture and attention configuration. This controlled design isolates the effect of attention from confounds such as training data or optimization differences. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline (pure attention) at both scales.
8	
9	\subsection{Task Taxonomy}\label{ssec:task_taxonomy}
10	
11	We evaluate on 10 benchmarks grouped into four categories defined by the primary cognitive demand of each task. This taxonomy (informal; not pre-registered) was fixed prior to analysis following established distinctions in the hybrid architecture literature \citep{arora2024zoology} (see Appendix~\ref{app:benchmark_details} for the confirmatory/exploratory split), not derived post hoc from observed results. The categories test whether the effect of attention on cross-architecture variance depends on task type.
12

_source line 4851 · 2026-05-24T12:35:47.638107+00:00_

9	We present the first systematic per-example analysis of how attention affects cross-architecture agreement in hybrid recurrent models. Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, we compare three architectures---GatedDeltaNet (gated delta rules), RetNet (multi-scale exponential decay), and HGRN2 (gated state expansion)---at five attention ratios (0--25\%) and two scales (340M, 1.3B). We evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks spanning retrieval, comprehension, reasoning, and mixed categories. On four benchmarks (LAMBADA, $N = 5{,}153$; HellaSwag, $N = 10{,}042$; PIQA, $N = 1{,}838$; ARC-Easy, $N = 2{,}376$) we conduct per-example decompositions, classifying each example as helped, hurt, or unchanged when attention is added, and measure cross-architecture overlap via Jaccard similarity. Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling} previews our central result: aggregate variance and per-example overlap follow independent trajectories.
10

1	# FLA 版本与 m-a-p 模型兼容性调查结果 (2026-05-18)
2	
3	## 核心结论：fla 0.5.0 与 m-a-p 模型兼容，attn.D 丢弃无影响
4	
5	（详情见前版，略）
6	
7	- m-a-p 模型的 `attn.D` 是死参数，fla ≥0.1.0 已移除，strict=False 加载安全
8	- 训练框架：fla + flame (torchtitan)，训练数据：FineWeb-Edu，72 模型
9	- HuggingFace: https://huggingface.co/collections/m-a-p/hybrid-linear-attention-research-686c488a63d609d2f20e2b1e
10	
11	---
12	
13	# 统计报告规则 (2026-05-19, Director 硬约束)
14	
15	**所有统计 claim 必须附原始数值 (ρ=X, p=Y)。** "majority"/"most" 必须有准确计数。适用于所有产出。
16	
17	---
18	
19	# exp-003 表面特征：无法区分 helped/hurt (2026-05-19)
20	
21	表面特征（token frequency、context length、lexical diversity）对 LAMBADA helped/hurt 无区分力（GDN/HGRN2 全部 p>0.2）。机制在模型内部，不在输入。→ 需要 exp-004 attention weight analysis。
22	
23	---
24	
25	# exp-004-r2 Attention Patterns：不是 per-example 效应的来源 (2026-05-19)
26	
27	**Attention entropy 无法区分 helped vs hurt**（三个架构差异均 <0.02）。Per-example divergence 不由 attention pattern 差异驱动——效应发生在 recurrent component 层面。
28	
29	**跨架构相似度发现**：GDN-RetNet 全层高度相似（>0.9）。HGRN2 在浅层 L2 与另外两者差异大（cos sim ~0.2），但深层收敛到 >0.94。→ 三架构深层 attention 相似但 per-example 仍分化，进一步支持 decoupling。
30	
31	**论文影响**：排除了 "不同 attention pattern → 不同受影响样本" 的 trivial 解释。效应来源指向 attention-recurrence interaction，非 attention 本身。Supplementary/appendix material。
32	
33	---
34	
35	# exp-004 Attention 层结构 (2026-05-19)
36	
37	3 架构 h3-1 都有 8 个 attention 层，索引 [2,5,8,11,14,17,20,23]。16 heads (8 KV heads, GQA)。Hook point: `model.model.layers[i].attn`。Flash Attention 不返回 weights，需 forward hook 截获 Q,K 手动计算 softmax(QK^T/√d)。
38	
39	---
40	
41	# exp-007 lm-eval 脚本选择：必须用 run_lmeval_clean.py (2026-05-19, 修正)
42	
43	**根因不是 dtype，是 `allow_neg_eigval=True` 配置注入缺失。**
44	
45	GDN 模型的 config.json 有 `"use_beta": true`。`run_lmeval_clean.py` 第 44-46 行有 `if getattr(config, "use_beta", False): config.allow_neg_eigval = True`，这改变了 GatedDeltaNet 的 forward pass。`run_lm_eval_fla.py` 缺少此步骤 → acc=0.3423 vs 正确 0.3753（~3.3pp 偏差）。
46	
47	**规则**：所有 fla 模型评估必须用 `run_lmeval_clean.py`，不要另写 wrapper。它已支持 `--log_samples`、`--tasks`、HGRN2 monkey-patch、`allow_neg
…[truncated 2387 chars]

1	## 当前项目
2	当前绑定项目：**entrainment_gated_ft**
3	
4	## 按需查阅
5	- 项目配置：`load_project_config("entrainment_gated_ft")` 或 `load_project_config("entrainment_gated_ft", section="execution")`
6	- 知识文件：`read_project_file(file=...)` 读取项目文件（ROADMAP.md/memory.md/ideas.md/report.md 等）
7	- 路线图：`read_roadmap()` 读取研究方向和短期任务
8	- 产出目录：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/artifacts/`（写文件后用 `register_artifact` 注册）
9	
10	## 用户自定义规则（必须遵守）
11	### 审稿规则
12	
13	- **[EMNLP 2026]** “EMNLP 2026 通过 ARR 统一审稿，Main/Findings 不分开投稿（不是两个Track），Long/Short 也走同一审稿系统，Overall Assessment的评分标准如下：5 = Consider for Award（约前2.5%），4.5 = Borderline Award，4 = Conference（可被主会议接收），3.5 = Borderline Conference，3 = Findings（可被Findings接收），2.5 = Borderline Findings，2 = Resubmit next cycle（需大幅修改，可在下一周期完成），1.5 = Resubmit after next cycle（需大幅修改，无法在下一周期完成），1 = Do not resubmit（需完全重做或与*ACL无关）”
14	- **[EMNLP 2026]** EMNLP 2026 restricts long paper submissions to a strict 8-page limit for main text, with unlimited space for references. Mandatory limitations and optional ethical considerations do not count towards this page limit, provided they do not contain excessive additional content.
15	
16	### 写作规则
17	
18	- **[EMNLP 2026]** Limitations 章节只需要写一小段，覆盖 3 个点左右就可以了，不需要写得很长
19	- **[EMNLP 2026]** ARR Responsible NLP Checklist在OpenReview的submission form里面（由用户手动填写），而不是在论文pdf里面
20	- **[EMNLP 2026]** 一篇文章只能有一个limitations section，放在正文里面，appendix不能有 limitations section
21	- **[EMNLP 2026]** abstract 不要超过 200 words
22	
23	> 以上规则由用户配置，优先级等同于硬约束。违反会被 Reviewer 标记。
24	
25	## Worker 操作规范
26	
27	### ssh-env-setup
28	
29	# 远程服务器环境搭建规范
30	
31	## 核心原则
32	
33	**先探测，再安装。** 不要假设服务器上什么都没有——很多包已经装好了。安装前必须走 discovery 流程。
34	
35	**镜像快速路径**：连接服务器后，先用 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果是已知镜像且版本匹配，跳过基础环境搭建（Phase 1-4），直接安装项目特有依赖。未检测到镜像时走传统流程。
36	
37	**aml-train 镜像使用 conda 环境**（不是 venv）。环境激活：`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`。pip/python 在 `/root/miniconda3/bin/` 下。**不要假设 .venv 存在，不要创建 venv。**

_source line 4905 · 2026-05-24T12:47:34.551672+00:00_

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.
4	
5	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}
6	
7	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, which provides controlled comparisons across architectures and attention ratios. The zoo contains three linear recurrent architectures: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2}. These three architectures represent distinct approaches to linear recurrence: GatedDeltaNet uses gated delta rules, RetNet uses multi-scale exponential decay, and HGRN2 uses gated linear recurrence with state expansion. Each architecture is trained at five attention ratios: 0\% (pure recurrent), 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, and 25\%. We adopt the h$k$-1 notation from the original work, where one attention layer is interleaved per every $k{+}1$ total layers: h3-1 corresponds to 25\% attention, h6-1 to 14.3\%, h12-1 to 7.7\%, and h24-1 to 4\%. All configurations are available at two scales: 340M and 1.3B parameters. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters; they differ only in architecture and attention configuration. This controlled design isolates the effect of attention from confounds such as training data or optimization differences. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline (pure attention) at both scales.

## P0 Critical（必须做）

### 1. 引用 Benchmark Illusion + 重新界定 "first" claim
- 添加到 references.bib:
```bibtex
@article{yang2026benchmark,
  title={Benchmark Illusion: Disagreement among {LLMs} and Its Scientific Consequences},
  author={Yang, Eddie and Wang, Dashun},
  journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2602.11898},
  year={2026}
}
```
- Related Work §"Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity" 段末添加：Yang & Wang (2026) demonstrate this phenomenon at the frontier-LLM level, showing 16–66% per-item disagreement despite comparable benchmark accuracy; our work provides a controlled within-family analysis that isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences.
- Introduction: "the first systematic per-example analysis" → "the first controlled within-family per-example analysis"（区分我们的 contribution：controlled model zoo，同 training regime，vs Yang & Wang 的 frontier LLMs 跨训练体系）
- Contribution 列表 item 1 也做同样修改

### 2. Contribution 2 重构：Jaccard 饱和替代 Decoupling
核心逻辑：decoupling 叙事（aggregate variance decreases while overlap stays flat）建立在 p=.170 非显著前提上，已被连续 6 轮审稿 flag。替代方案：Jaccard 饱和（4% attention 即达完整 pattern）有跨 4 个 benchmark 的一致证据，更强。

修改点：
- §4.2 标题："Aggregate Variance and Per-Example Overlap" → "Per-Example Overlap Saturates at Minimal Attention"
- §4.2 opening：重构为"Jaccard 在 4% attention 即饱和，额外 attention 不再改变 per-example pattern"为主叙事。Aggregate variance 的 non-significant decreasing trend 作为背景观察而非 claim。
- Introduction 第二个贡献 bullet：重写为 Jaccard saturation finding（4% attention 即确立完整 per-example divergence pattern；additional attention 改变 aggregate gap 但不改变哪些 example 被影响）
- Figure 1 caption 调整：强调 (b) Jaccard 饱和为核心发现，(a) aggregate variance 为背景
- Conclusion 对应段落同步更新

### 3. Abstract 统一 pass
在 #1 和 #2 完成后，重写 Abstract（保持 ≤200 words）：
- 开头用"architectures interchangeable by aggregate metrics differ per-example"框架
- Core finding 1: multiplicity varies by task type (α=0.14–0.30)
- Core finding 2: Jaccard saturates at first attention level (4%)
- Routing 只用 1 句话提及（"preliminary routing exploits complementarity on high-multiplicity tasks"）
- 引入 Yang & Wang 在 LLMs 上类似发现的对比
- 不包含 "decoupling" 一词

## P1 Major（应该做）

### 4. Format confound (R17-W1)
- §4.1: 每次出现 "task type" 改为 "task type (or evaluation format)"（首次出现）或类似限定
- Limitations 加 1 句："The confound between task type and evaluation format cannot be fully resolved: LAMBADA is the only free-form benchmark, so its higher α may reflect format differences rather than cognitive demands alone."

### 5. GDN flag (R6-W2)

- §4.2 或 Table 1 附近：补一句 MMLU ρ reversal when excluding GDN

### 6. Routing qualifier (R15-W2)
- Abstract 中 routing 相关句子加 "preliminary" + "task-dependent"
- §4.4（或 routing 段落）末尾："A natural comparison—average-logits ensemble—is left to future work as it requires simultaneous inference across all architectures."

### 7. Scale 语言 (R16-W4)
- 明确区分：per-example Jaccard consistent across both scales (340M, 1.3B) vs aggregate variance trend observed only at 1.3B on LAMBADA

### 8. 5-arch scope (R16-W2)

_source line 4933 · 2026-05-24T13:50:11.155505+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures---including GDN, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, and DeltaNet---achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets. Yet they can differ on up to 90\% of the individual examples they affect: adding attention to three controlled linear recurrent backbones yields helped and hurt sets with at most 10\% pairwise overlap, approximately twice the independence baseline. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics: architectures that score within 1--2 points of each other reach similar accuracy by gaining and losing on different inputs. Across all ten pairwise comparisons among five architectures, overlap in per-example effects remains below 14\% (all $p < .01$), meaning the choice of backbone is associated with which specific inputs a deployed system handles correctly.
4	
5	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. These systems now serve as production-grade alternatives to pure Transformers, and the practical question of how much attention to include is typically answered by comparing aggregate benchmark numbers. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide a controlled model zoo spanning three recurrent architectures, five attention ratios, and two parameter scales, which enables systematic study of how attention allocation affects these hybrid designs.
6	
7	Prior work on predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} and underspecification \citep{damour2022underspecification,mccoy2020berts} documents this risk for seed variation within a single architecture, but whether it extends to architectural variation in hybrid 
…[truncated 4111 chars]

_source line 4935 · 2026-05-24T13:50:11.657990+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} and Hyena \citep{poli2023hyena} establish early design points in the hybrid space with gated convolutions and subquadratic operators. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear a
…[truncated 2557 chars]

_source line 4939 · 2026-05-24T13:50:13.063055+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose, with pairwise Jaccard uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$) and saturated at the first attention level. Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	Two observations follow at the 340M--1.3B scales studied here. First, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Second, the low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), but our preliminary routing experiments suggest that exploiting it is task-dependent: confidence-based selection shows gains on high-multiplicity free-form tasks (LAMBADA: $+5.2$\,pp) but is counterproductive on low-multiplicity ones (PIQA: $-0.49$\,pp).
7

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.
4	
5	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}
6	
7	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, which provides controlled comparisons across architectures and attention ratios. The zoo contains three linear recurrent architectures: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2}. These three architectures represent distinct approaches to linear recurrence: GatedDeltaNet uses gated delta rules, RetNet uses multi-scale exponential decay, and HGRN2 uses gated linear recurrence with state expansion. Each architecture is trained at five attention ratios: 0\% (pure recurrent), 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, and 25\%. We adopt the h$k$-1 notation from the original work, where one attention layer is interleaved per every $k{+}1$ total layers: h3-1 corresponds to 25\% attention, h6-1 to 14.3\%, h12-1 to 7.7\%, and h24-1 to 4\%. All configurations are available at two scales: 340M and 1.3B parameters. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters; they differ only in architecture and attention configuration. This controlled design isolates the effect of attention from confounds such as training data or optimization differences. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline (pure attention) at both scales.

38	\begin{abstract}
39	Practitioners select among hybrid linear recurrent architectures using aggregate benchmarks, but architectures that appear interchangeable by these metrics can differ on which specific examples they get right. Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that adding attention is associated with substantially different per-example changes across architectures (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) more than attention dose. _source line 4963 · 2026-05-24T13:54:12.908738+00:00_

735	
736	Three caveats qualify this comparison. First, scale mismatch: Pythia 70M is $5\times$ smaller than our smallest models (340M); attempts to use Pythia 410M failed repeatedly. Second, architecture family: Pythia is a standard Transformer, not a hybrid recurrent architecture, so this is a cross-family reference point rather than a matched control. Third, accuracy regime: Pythia 70M accuracy (LAMBADA 0.17--0.23, ARC-Easy ${\sim}$0.38) is far below our models (${\sim}$0.50, ${\sim}$0.72), and Jaccard is affected by base rates; the 2.7--4.3$\times$ ratio may differ at accuracy regimes closer to our main experiments. These limitations restrict the comparison to a reference point for seed-variation magnitude rather than definitive evidence for architecture-driven effects.
737	
738	\section{Extended Architecture Validation (Five Architectures)}\label{app:extended_arch}
739	
740	To test whether the low cross-architecture Jaccard pattern generalizes beyond the three primary architectures, we extend the analysis to include two additional architectures from the same model zoo: GLA (Gated Linear Attention) and DeltaNet, yielding ten pairwise comparisons. Both are available only at 340M; all results below use the h3-1 (25\% attention) configuration.

```json
{
```
```json
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item Architectural variation---a source of multiplicity not previously characterized at the per-example level---is associated with structured predictive multiplicity ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) across four benchmarks, with magnitude that depends on task type rather than attention dose (\\S\\ref{ssec:per_example}, Table~\\ref{tab:multiplicity}). Validation across five architectures confirms the pattern: all ten pairwise Jaccard values remain $\\leq 0.14$ (all $p < .01$; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_arch}). The primary controlled variable is the recurrent backbone \\citep[cf.][]{mccoy2020berts, damour2022underspecification}, though single training runs prevent full disentanglement from seed effects (Limitations).\n    \\item Per-example Jaccard saturates at the first attention level (4\\%) across all four benchmarks, while aggregate variance shows a non-significant decreasing pattern on LAMBADA (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_bench_jaccard}). Minimal attention suffices to establish the full pattern of configuration-specific per-example effects; additional attention narrows aggregate gaps without homogenizing which examples each architecture affects. The aggregate variance pattern depends on GDN inclusion (\\S\\ref{ssec:robustness}) and does not replicate on other benchmarks.\n\\end{itemize}",
  "new_string": "\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item Architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models---controlled within a single model zoo sharing training data and hyperparameters---produces structured predictive multiplicity whose magnitude varies by task type (or evaluation format) rather than attention dose (\\S\\ref{ssec:per_example}). Five-architecture validation confirms the pattern (\\S\\ref{ssec:robustness}; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_arch}).\n    \\item Per-example overlap saturates at the first attention level (4\\%) across all four benchmarks: minimal attention establishes which examples each architecture affects, and additional attention does not homogenize these per-example sets (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}).\n\\end{itemize}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/main.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "Practitioners select among hybrid linear recurrent architectures using aggregate benchmarks, but architectures that appear interchangeable by these metrics can differ on which specific examples they get right. Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that adding attention is associated with substantially different per-example changes across architectures (Jaccard $\\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) more than attention dose. Per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat, even as aggregate variance shows a non-significant decreasing trend on one benchmark (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$). Majority vote fails to exploit the resulting complementarity. Confidence-informed routing extracts 36\\% of oracle complementarity on LAMBADA ($+5.2$\\,pp), suggesting these differences are partially actionable on high-multiplicity tasks.",
  "new_string": "Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \\citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. Per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, indicating that minimal attention establishes which examples each architecture affects. \\citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\\%) among frontier LLMs despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (AUC 0.70--0.74), and preliminary task-dependent routing extracts 36\\% of oracle complementarity on LAMBADA ($+5.2$\\,pp) but is counterproductive on low-multiplicity tasks."
}
```

</details>

70	
71	\subsection{Per-Example Overlap Saturates at Minimal Attention}\label{ssec:decoupling}
72	
73	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard saturates at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), indicating that minimal attention suffices to establish the full per-example divergence pattern. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$).
74

76	\centering
77	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_task_specificity.pdf}
78	\caption{Spearman $\rho$ between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\sigma$ for each benchmark at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA ($\rho = -1.0$; BCa 95\% CI $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, heuristic) is the only benchmark with a negative correlation whose confidence interval excludes zero. Benchmarks are grouped by task category. Bootstrap CI is heuristic given $n = 3$ architectures.}

Upper triangle: $J_{\text{helped}}$; lower triangle: $J_{\text{hurt}}$. All values $\leq 0.14$, all permutation tests $p \leq .002$. HellaSwag and PIQA results in Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}.}

_source line 5001 · 2026-05-24T13:56:52.177769+00:00_

38	\begin{abstract}
39	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. Per-example overlap saturates at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, indicating that minimal attention establishes which examples each architecture affects. \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier LLMs despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (AUC 0.70--0.74), and preliminary task-dependent routing extracts 36\% of oracle complementarity on LAMBADA ($+5.2$\,pp) but is counterproductive on low-multiplicity tasks.
40	\end{abstract}

[Director 指令] [Patch — R20 最终修改，DDL 明天]

目标：稳固 Findings 3.0 + 消除降分 flag。不追求提分到 3.5（reviewer 确认需 evidence 不可能靠写作达到）。引用已验证，不需再查。

## P0（必须做）

### 1. Routing 结构性降级（W4，复发 5 轮，qualifier 策略已失败）
- **Abstract**: 当前 routing 句缩为 ~12 词："Preliminary routing partially exploits this on one benchmark but is counterproductive on others."
- **Conclusion**: 删除 routing 作为独立 observation。改为单段式——per-example audit 为唯一 named implication，routing 融入 complementarity："Low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity (+7–14pp oracle gain), though preliminary routing experiments show exploitation remains task-dependent (+5.2pp on LAMBADA; −0.49pp on PIQA)."
- 不要完全删除 routing（审稿人可能反问"为什么不试 exploit"）

### 2. "attention dose" → "attention ratio" 全文替换
全文 grep "attention dose"，全部替换为 "attention ratio"。应有约 7 处（Abstract、Intro contribution、§4.1、Conclusion、Appendix ×3）。

## P1（应该做）

### 3. 替代解读讨论（W10，新 Info 级但有深度价值）
§4.2 Jaccard saturation 段之后加 2-3 句：
"An alternative interpretation is that saturation reflects rapid adoption of Transformer-like behavior rather than architecture-specific fingerprints being locked in at minimal attention: Appendix I shows that h3-1 correct sets converge toward the Transformer baseline. Disentangling these accounts requires ablation of the attention mechanism itself, which we leave to future work."

### 4. 补 Geifman + Caruana 引用（W6）
bib 添加：
```bibtex
@inproceedings{geifman2017selective,
  title={Selective Classification for Deep Neural Networks},
  author={Geifman, Yonatan and El-Yaniv, Ran},
  booktitle={Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems},
  year={2017}
}

@inproceedings{caruana2004ensemble,
  title={Ensemble Selection from Libraries of Models},
  author={Caruana, Rich and Niculescu-Mizil, Alexandru and Crew, Geoff and Ksikes, Alex},
  booktitle={Proceedings of the 21st International Conference on Machine Learning},
  year={2004}
}
```
Related Work routing/ensemble 相关段加引用："Our routing approach relates to selective prediction \citep{geifman2017selective} and ensemble selection \citep{caruana2004ensemble}, though we select among architecturally distinct models rather than same-architecture ensembles."

### 5. GDN Table 1 脚注（W2）
Table 1 caption 末尾加脚注："Excluding GDN reverses the MMLU $\rho$ from $-0.7$ to $+0.6$; see \S4.3 for robustness analysis."

### 6. Permutation difficulty caveat（W9）
§3.5 或 Limitations 加 1 句："The permutation null does not condition on item difficulty; shared-difficulty effects may inflate baseline overlap expectations."

### 7. Greedy decoding（W7）
Limitations 加 1 句："All evaluations use greedy decoding (the lm-eval-harness default); sampling strategies may alter per-example agreement patterns."

## 约束
- 正文 ≤ 8 页，Abstract ≤ 200 words
- 修改完 paper_compile_and_check 确认编译通过
- Page 8 如果仍拥挤（3 figures），考虑将 Figure 7 移到 Appendix

38	\begin{abstract}
39	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. _source line 5027 · 2026-05-24T14:38:01.507938+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. Per-example overlap saturates at 4\% attention (pairwise Jaccard $\leq 0.10$), establishing architecture-specific sensitivity patterns that additional attention does not alter. Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	Two observations follow at the 340M--1.3B scales studied here. First, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Second, the low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), but our preliminary routing experiments suggest that exploiting it is task-dependent: confidence-based selection shows gains on high-multiplicity free-form tasks (LAMBADA: $+5.2$\,pp) but is counterproductive on low-multiplicity ones (PIQA: $-0.49$\,pp).
7

```json
  "new_string": "\\vspace{-2pt}\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nWe have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention (pairwise Jaccard $\\leq 0.10$), establishing architecture-specific sensitivity patterns that additional attention does not alter. Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\\%.\n\nAt the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\\,pp oracle gain), though preliminary routing experiments show exploitation remains task-dependent ($+5.2$\\,pp on LAMBADA; $-0.49$\\,pp on PIQA)."
}
```

</details>
